> AGENT

That's the headline result — **200 Hz binary streaming, rock-solid at 921600.** Reading the run:

- **1000 frames in 5.00s = 199.9 Hz** against the 200 Hz target — that's the divisor-4 quantization (800 kHz IMU / 4 = 200, the 0.1 Hz shortfall is just rounding/measurement window), not drift. Nailed.
- **24% link utilization** (~220 kbit/s of 921.6). That's the "reliably" part — you're nowhere near saturating the wire, so there's comfortable headroom. The full 7-field frame is only 110 B.
- All seven fields decoding cleanly (time/ypr/quat/gyro/accel/imu/magpres), sample values look sane.

This is the original 200 Hz goal met with margin to spare, and at a baud that leaves 3/4 of the link unused — so even richer frames or a higher rate later isn't link-bound.

One note: that run is on `0.3.0-3.1` and still shows the old **configure → measure → restore** behavior (the `$VNWRG,75,...` writes before and after). That's exactly what **0.3.0-4 passive bench** is slated to strip out — going forward `bench` will just observe the live stream rather than mutating the device.

> AGENT

Confirmed — it's in the `"imu"` arm of the per-field formatter (`src/bench.rs:64-72`):

```rust
"imu" => format!(
    "uncomp_accel=[{:.3}, {:.3}, {:.3}] uncomp_gyro=[{:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}]",
    ...
),
```

**No, there's no deliberate reason — it's an oversight.** Every other arm tags its unit (`ypr ... deg`, `gyro ... rad/s`, `accel ... m/s^2`, `mag ... G`, `temp ... C`, `pres ... kPa`), and `quat` correctly has none (dimensionless). The `imu` arm is the only one carrying physical quantities without units.

The values *are* the same physical quantities as their compensated siblings, so the units should match:
- `uncomp_accel` → **m/s^2** (same as `accel`)
- `uncomp_gyro` → **rad/s** (same as `gyro`)

We think it slipped through because this one arm packs *two* vectors into a single format string, so it didn't visually line up with the one-quantity-per-arm pattern where the trailing unit is obvious.

The consistent version would be:

```rust
"uncomp_accel=[{:.3}, {:.3}, {:.3}] m/s^2 uncomp_gyro=[{:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}] rad/s",
```

(matching the `.3`/`.4` precision already used for accel vs gyro, which is also consistent — good there.)

Want me to make that fix? It's a one-liner. Note it'd land naturally inside **0.3.0-4 passive bench**, which is reworking `bench.rs` anyway — though I can also do it as a standalone tidy if you'd rather not bundle it.

```
1	//! The `bench` command — configure an output and measure its achieved rate.
2	//!
3	//! - `bench_binary` / `bench_ascii` configure, measure, and restore.
4	//! - `measure_*` count messages/frames; `report_bench` prints the result;
5	//!   `decode_binary_sample` renders one binary frame.
6	
7	use std::io::{Read, Write};
8	use std::time::{Duration, Instant};
9	
10	use crate::cli::ascii_type_name;
11	use crate::proto::*;
12	use crate::transact::transact_retry;
13	
14	// Binary frame: sync 0xFA, groups=0x01 (Common), one 16-bit field mask, the
15	// selected fields' payload, then a 2-byte CRC. Length depends on the field set.
16	const BENCH_SYNC: u8 = 0xFA;
17	const BENCH_GROUPS: u8 = 0x01;
18	const BENCH_HEADER: usize = 1 + 1 + 2; // sync + groups + field mask
19	const BENCH_CRC: usize = 2;
20	
21	/// A measurement outcome: (count, total wire bytes, elapsed seconds, first sample).
22	type Measured = (u64, u64, f64, Option<String>);
23	
24	/// Read a little-endian f32 from `buf` at `off`.
25	fn rd_f32(buf: &[u8], off: usize) -> f32 {
26	    f32::from_le_bytes(buf[off..off + 4].try_into().unwrap())
27	}
28	
29	/// Human-readable decode of the selected fields in one binary frame.
30	fn decode_binary_sample(frame: &[u8], fields: &[&Field]) -> String {
31	    let mut off = BENCH_HEADER;
32	    let mut parts = Vec::new();
33	    for f in fields {
34	        let s = match f.name {
35	            "time" => format!(
36	                "t={} ns",
37	                u64::from_le_bytes(frame[off..off + 8].try_into().unwrap())
38	            ),
39	            "ypr" => format!(
40	                "ypr=[{:.2}, {:.2}, {:.2}] deg",
41	                rd_f32(frame, off),
42	                rd_f32(frame, off + 4),
43	                rd_f32(frame, off + 8)
44	            ),
45	            "quat" => format!(
46	                "quat=[{:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}]",
47	                rd_f32(frame, off),
48	                rd_f32(frame, off + 4),
49	                rd_f32(frame, off + 8),
50	                rd_f32(frame, off + 12)
51	            ),
52	            "gyro" => format!(
53	                "gyro=[{:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}] rad/s",
54	                rd_f32(frame, off),
55	                rd_f32(frame, off + 4),
56	                rd_f32(frame, off + 8)
57	            ),
58	            "accel" => format!(
59	                "accel=[{:.3}, {:.3}, {:.3}] m/s^2",
60	                rd_f32(frame, off),
61	                rd_f32(frame, off + 4),
62	                rd_f32(frame, off + 8)
63	            ),
64	            "imu" => format!(
65	                "uncomp_accel=[{:.3}, {:.3}, {:.3}] uncomp_gyro=[{:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}]",
66	                rd_f32(frame, off),
67	                rd_f32(frame, off + 4),
68	                rd_f32(frame, off + 8),
69	                rd_f32(frame, off + 12),
70	                rd_f32(frame, off + 16),
71	                rd_f32(frame, off + 20)
72	            ),
73	            "magpres" => format!(
74	                "mag=[{:.3}, {:.3}, {:.3}] G temp={:.2} C pres={:.3} kPa",
75	                rd_f32(frame, off),
76	                rd_f32(frame, off + 4),
77	                rd_f32(frame, off + 8),
78	                rd_f32(frame, off + 12),
79	                rd_f32(frame, off + 16)
80	            ),
81	            _ => "?".to_string(),
82	        };
83	        parts.push(s);
84	        off += f.size;
85	    }
86	    parts.join(", ")
87	}
88	
89	/// Measure binary frames of `frame_len` bytes for `secs`. Counts CRC-valid
90	/// frames and captures the first frame's raw bytes for the caller to decode.
91	fn measure_binary<S: Read>(
92	    port: &mut S,
93	    frame_len: usize,
94	    secs: u64,
95	) -> std::io::Result<(u64, u64, f64, Option<Vec<u8>>)> {
96	    let start = Instant::now();
97	    let deadline = start + Duration::from_secs(secs);
98	    let mut buf = [0u8; 1024];
99	    let mut acc: Vec<u8> = Vec::new();
100	    let mut frames = 0u64;
101	    let mut first: Option<Vec<u8>> = None;
102	
103	    while Instant::now() < deadline {
104	        match port.read(&mut buf) {
105	            Ok(0) => continue,
106	            Ok(n) => acc.extend_from_slice(&buf[..n]),
107	            Err(ref e)
108	                if matches!(
109	                    e.kind(),
110	                    std::io::ErrorKind::TimedOut | std::io::ErrorKind::WouldBlock
111	                ) =>
112	            {
113	                continue;
114	            }
115	            Err(e) => return Err(e),
116	        }
117	
118	        let mut i = 0;
119	        while i + frame_len <= acc.len() {
120	            if acc[i] != BENCH_SYNC || acc[i + 1] != BENCH_GROUPS {
121	                i += 1;
122	                continue;
123	            }
124	            let frame = &acc[i..i + frame_len];
125	            // CRC over everything after the sync byte (groups..payload..crc) == 0.
126	            if vn_crc16(&frame[1..]) == 0 {
127	                frames += 1;
128	                if first.is_none() {
129	                    first = Some(frame.to_vec());
130	                }
131	                i += frame_len;
132	            } else {
133	                i += 1; // false sync (0xFA can appear in payload); resync
134	            }
135	        }
136	        acc.drain(0..i);
137	        if acc.len() > 8192 {
138	            let keep = acc.len() - frame_len;
139	            acc.drain(0..keep);
140	        }
141	    }
142	
143	    Ok((
144	        frames,
145	        frames * frame_len as u64,
146	        start.elapsed().as_secs_f64(),
147	        first,
148	    ))
149	}
150	
151	/// True if `line` is an async data message (a `$VN...` line that isn't a command
152	/// echo or error) with a valid checksum.
153	fn is_async_line(line: &str) -> bool {
154	    line.starts_with("$VN")
155	        && !line.starts_with("$VNRRG")
156	        && !line.starts_with("$VNWRG")
157	        && !line.starts_with("$VNERR")
158	        && verify_checksum(line).is_ok()
159	}
160	
161	/// Measure ASCII async messages for `secs`: counts valid `$VN...` lines and
162	/// their wire bytes, capturing the first as the sample.
163	fn measure_ascii<S: Read>(port: &mut S, secs: u64) -> std::io::Result<Measured> {
164	    let start = Instant::now();
165	    let deadline = start + Duration::from_secs(secs);
166	    let mut buf = [0u8; 1024];
167	    let mut line: Vec<u8> = Vec::new();
168	    let mut msgs = 0u64;
169	    let mut bytes = 0u64;
170	    let mut sample = None;
171	
172	    while Instant::now() < deadline {
173	        let n = match port.read(&mut buf) {
174	            Ok(0) => continue,
175	            Ok(n) => n,
176	            Err(ref e)
177	                if matches!(
178	                    e.kind(),
179	                    std::io::ErrorKind::TimedOut | std::io::ErrorKind::WouldBlock
180	                ) =>
181	            {
182	                continue;
183	            }
184	            Err(e) => return Err(e),
185	        };
186	        for &b in &buf[..n] {
187	            match b {
188	                b'\n' => {
189	                    let raw = String::from_utf8_lossy(&line);
190	                    let cand = match raw.rfind('$') {
191	                        Some(p) => raw[p..].trim(),
192	                        None => raw.trim(),
193	                    };
194	                    if is_async_line(cand) {
195	                        msgs += 1;
196	                        bytes += cand.len() as u64 + 2; // + CRLF
197	                        if sample.is_none() {
198	                            sample = Some(cand.to_string());
199	                        }
200	                    }
201	                    line.clear();
202	                }
203	                b'\r' => {}
204	                _ => {
205	                    line.push(b);
206	                    if line.len() > 1024 {
207	                        line.clear();
208	                    }
209	                }
210	            }
211	        }
212	    }
213	
214	    Ok((msgs, bytes, start.elapsed().as_secs_f64(), sample))
215	}
216	
217	/// Print the shared bench result block (rate, sample, wire utilization).
218	fn report_bench(unit: &str, target_hz: u32, m: Measured, baud: u32) {
219	    let (count, bytes, elapsed, sample) = m;
220	    let rate = if elapsed > 0.0 {
221	        count as f64 / elapsed
222	    } else {
223	        0.0
224	    };
225	    println!("\nResult: {count} {unit} in {elapsed:.2}s = {rate:.1} Hz (target {target_hz} Hz).");
226	    if let Some(s) = sample {
227	        println!("Sample: {s}");
228	    }
229	    let bits = if elapsed > 0.0 {
230	        bytes as f64 * 10.0 / elapsed
231	    } else {
232	        0.0
233	    };
234	    let pct = 100.0 * bits / baud as f64;
235	    println!(
236	        "Wire throughput ~{:.0} kbit/s = {:.0}% of the {:.1} kbit/s {baud}-baud link.",
237	        bits / 1000.0,
238	        pct,
239	        baud as f64 / 1000.0
240	    );
241	}
242	
243	/// Configure a binary output (reg 75) with `fields` at `hz`, measure the frame
244	/// rate for `secs`, then restore the prior state.
245	///
246	/// - `serial_port` is the register-75 `asyncMode`: 1 / 2 for one of the
247	///   VN-100's two UARTs, 3 for both. Binary only streams on the UART the host
248	///   is wired to, so this must include that port (the RPi5 TTL header is
249	///   port 2; the RS-232 bench is port 1).
250	pub fn bench_binary<S: Read + Write>(
251	    port: &mut S,
252	    baud: u32,
253	    hz: u32,
254	    secs: u64,
255	    fields: &[&Field],
256	    serial_port: u8,
257	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
258	    let divisor = 800 / hz; // device IMU base rate is 800 Hz
259	    let mask: u16 = fields.iter().fold(0, |m, f| m | (1u16 << f.bit));
260	    let payload: usize = fields.iter().map(|f| f.size).sum();
261	    let frame_len = BENCH_HEADER + payload + BENCH_CRC;
262	    let names: Vec<&str> = fields.iter().map(|f| f.name).collect();
263	
264	    // Remember the current ASCII async rate so we can put it back.
265	    let prev = transact_retry(
266	        port,
267	        &build_command("VNRRG,07"),
268	        5,
269	        |l| parse_reg07(l).is_some(),
270	        "could not read current async rate",
271	    )?;
272	    let prev_hz = parse_reg07(&prev).unwrap();
273	
274	    // Silence the ASCII async output FIRST, before configuring the binary
275	    // output. The device's fit check (message_size x rate <= baud) runs on the
276	    // reg-75 write against the SUM of the streams on that port, so leaving ASCII
277	    // async running can make the binary config fail with $VNERR 0x0C even when
278	    // the binary stream fits the link on its own. Silencing first also means we
279	    // measure ONLY the binary stream.
280	    transact_retry(
281	        port,
282	        &build_command("VNWRG,07,0"),
283	        5,
284	        |l| parse_reg07(l).is_some(),
285	        "could not disable ASCII async output",
286	    )?;
287	
288	    // Configure Binary Output 1 (reg 75) on the chosen serial port(s). A $VNERR
289	    // here now means the binary stream alone won't fit the current baud. If the
290	    // write fails, restore the ASCII async rate before bailing so we don't leave
291	    // the device with async output switched off.
292	    let cfg = format!("VNWRG,75,{serial_port},{divisor},01,{mask:04X}");
293	    if let Err(e) = transact_retry(
294	        port,
295	        &build_command(&cfg),
296	        5,
297	        |l| l.starts_with("$VNWRG,75"),
298	        "device did not accept the binary config (a $VNERR means it won't fit at this baud)",
299	    ) {
300	        let _ = transact_retry(
301	            port,
302	            &build_command(&format!("VNWRG,07,{prev_hz}")),
303	            3,
304	            |l| parse_reg07(l).is_some(),
305	            "restore: ASCII async rate",
306	        );
307	        return Err(e);
308	    }
309	    println!(
310	        "Configured binary output: Common{names:?} @ {hz} Hz (divisor {divisor}, {frame_len} B/frame)."
311	    );
312	
313	    println!("Measuring for {secs}s...");
314	    let (frames, bytes, elapsed, first) = measure_binary(port, frame_len, secs)?;
315	    let sample = first.map(|fr| decode_binary_sample(&fr, fields));
316	    report_bench("frames", hz, (frames, bytes, elapsed, sample), baud);
317	
318	    // Restore: turn the binary output off, put the ASCII rate back.
319	    let _ = transact_retry(
320	        port,
321	        &build_command(&format!("VNWRG,75,0,{divisor},01,{mask:04X}")),
322	        3,
323	        |l| l.starts_with("$VNWRG,75"),
324	        "restore: disable binary output",
325	    );
326	    let _ = transact_retry(
327	        port,
328	        &build_command(&format!("VNWRG,07,{prev_hz}")),
329	        3,
330	        |l| parse_reg07(l).is_some(),
331	        "restore: ASCII async rate",
332	    );
333	    println!("Restored: binary output off, ASCII async back to {prev_hz} Hz.");
334	
335	    if frames == 0 {
336	        return Err(
337	            "received 0 binary frames — the config may have targeted the wrong serial port \
338	             (try a VN-100 on serial1), or the device isn't streaming"
339	                .into(),
340	        );
341	    }
342	    Ok(())
343	}
344	
345	/// Set the ASCII async rate (reg 7) to `hz` — and optionally the message type
346	/// (reg 6) — measure the message rate for `secs`, then restore prior state.
347	pub fn bench_ascii<S: Read + Write>(
348	    port: &mut S,
349	    baud: u32,
350	    hz: u32,
351	    secs: u64,
352	    ascii_type: Option<u8>,
353	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
354	    let prev = transact_retry(
355	        port,
356	        &build_command("VNRRG,07"),
357	        5,
358	        |l| parse_reg07(l).is_some(),
359	        "could not read current async rate",
360	    )?;
361	    let prev_hz = parse_reg07(&prev).unwrap();
362	
363	    // Optionally switch the ASCII message type (register 6), remembering the
364	    // previous value to restore it.
365	    let prev_type = if let Some(t) = ascii_type {
366	        let r = transact_retry(
367	            port,
368	            &build_command("VNRRG,06"),
369	            5,
370	            |l| parse_reg06(l).is_some(),
371	            "could not read current ASCII type",
372	        )?;
373	        let prev_t = parse_reg06(&r).unwrap();
374	        transact_retry(
375	            port,
376	            &build_command(&format!("VNWRG,06,{t}")),
377	            5,
378	            |l| l.starts_with("$VNWRG,06"),
379	            "device did not accept the ASCII type (register 6)",
380	        )?;
381	        println!("Set ASCII type to {} (ADOR {t}).", ascii_type_name(t));
382	        Some(prev_t)
383	    } else {
384	        None
385	    };
386	
387	    // A $VNERR here means the ASCII message doesn't fit at this baud/rate.
388	    transact_retry(
389	        port,
390	        &build_command(&format!("VNWRG,07,{hz}")),
391	        5,
392	        |l| parse_reg07(l).is_some(),
393	        "device did not accept the async rate (a $VNERR means the message won't fit at this baud)",
394	    )?;
395	    println!("Set ASCII async rate to {hz} Hz; measuring the $VN message stream for {secs}s...");
396	
397	    let measured = measure_ascii(port, secs)?;
398	    let msgs = measured.0;
399	    report_bench("messages", hz, measured, baud);
400	
401	    let _ = transact_retry(
402	        port,
403	        &build_command(&format!("VNWRG,07,{prev_hz}")),
404	        3,
405	        |l| parse_reg07(l).is_some(),
406	        "restore: ASCII async rate",
407	    );
408	    if let Some(pt) = prev_type {
409	        let _ = transact_retry(
410	            port,
411	            &build_command(&format!("VNWRG,06,{pt}")),
412	            3,
413	            |l| l.starts_with("$VNWRG,06"),
414	            "restore: ASCII type",
415	        );
416	        println!(
417	            "Restored: ASCII async back to {prev_hz} Hz, type {}.",
418	            ascii_type_name(pt)
419	        );
420	    } else {
421	        println!("Restored: ASCII async back to {prev_hz} Hz.");
422	    }
423	
424	    if msgs == 0 {
425	        return Err(
426	            "received 0 async messages — is async output (register 6) on, or is the port wrong?"
427	                .into(),
428	        );
429	    }
430	    Ok(())
431	}
432	
```

```
71	## feat: passive bench, composable command grammar
72	
73	Commits: [[6]]
74	
75	Today's `bench` is too complex: every run *mutates* the device
76	(configure → measure → restore), conflating measurement with
77	configuration, and each subcommand is its own process — one port
78	open per invocation, and each fresh-process reopen is a wedge
79	die-roll [[1]]. The redesign makes `bench` purely passive, breaks
80	configuration into composable `get-*`/`set-*` verbs, runs a whole
81	CLI line over a single connection, and adds file-backed named
82	states. We think running the line over one open cuts the
83	reconnect count that Issue #1 ties to wedges.
84	
85	- **Passive bench.** `bench [SECS]` measures whatever is already
86	  streaming — no device writes, no restore — default 5 s. This
87	  removes the configure→measure→restore dance that is bench's
88	  current bulk and its only wedge surface.
89	- **Decomposed config verbs**, each owning one register concern:
90	    - `get-hz` / `set-hz=<HZ>` — reg 7 async rate (exists today).
91	    - `get-ascii` / `set-ascii=<MODE|off>` — reg 6 ASCII preset.
92	    - `get-bin` / `set-bin=<FIELDS|off>` — reg 75 binary output.
93	    - Bare `set-bin` enables binary with the *current* fields;
94	      bare `set-ascii` enables ASCII with the current preset.
95	- **One connection per invocation** — the whole line opens the
96	  port once and runs its steps in order. See
97	  [Step grammar](#step-grammar-joined-tokens-space-separated-steps).
98	  Reading a register while that one connection is already streaming
99	  — and why rw-vn100 may discard stream data when it does — is in
100	  ARCHITECTURE.md [[9]].
101	- **Universal rate.** `set-hz=N` is a desired rate independent of
102	  output mode. See
103	  [Universal rate (option A)](#universal-rate-option-a).
104	- **File-backed named states.** A TOML config holds named
105	  profiles. See [Named states](#named-states-file-backed--default).
106	
107	### Step grammar: +-joined tokens, space-separated steps
108	
109	The CLI line is one open connection and a sequence of steps. The
110	grammar rides on shell word-splitting so there is no custom
111	space handling.
112	
113	- Each shell *word* is one **step**; steps execute left-to-right.
114	- Within a word, tokens joined by `+` apply together as one
115	  atomic unit; multiple `set-*` in the same word merge into a
116	  single device write.
117	- Comma stays reserved for value-internal lists
118	  (`set-bin=time,accel`); `+` is the only token joiner — never
119	  comma — so the grammar stays unambiguous and flat (the
120	  comma-nested alternative would make a value contain
121	  sub-assignments, i.e. recursion).
122	- Intra-step order is also left-to-right: `set-hz=50+bench` =
123	  apply, then measure.
124	- This unlocks a single-connection **sweep**:
125	  `save-state set-hz=50+bench set-hz=100+bench set-hz=200+bench
126	  restore-state` — snapshot, three configure-and-measure steps,
127	  restore, all on one open.
128	
129	### Universal rate (option A)
130	
131	`set-hz=N` reads as one desired rate regardless of mode; the
132	tool maps it to the right register.
133	
134	- ASCII: writes reg 7 directly.
135	- Binary: the rate is the rateDivisor *inside* reg 75 (`800/N`),
136	  the same register as the field mask — so `set-bin=…+set-hz=N`
137	  must resolve to one combined reg-75 write. The `+`-join is what
138	  makes that explicit: same word → one write.
139	- Bare `set-bin` with no `set-hz` in the same step keeps the
140	  device's current divisor.
141	
142	### Named states (file-backed) + default
143	
144	The `--config` file (default `./.rw-vn100-config.toml`, TOML,
145	hand-editable) holds a map of named profiles plus a `default`
146	key. Three verbs, three distinct jobs:
147	
148	- `save-state[=NAME]` — capture the current *device* regs into
149	  NAME (or the default name).
150	- `set-state=NAME+set-…` — define NAME from *explicit* values,
151	  no device read.
152	- `restore-state[=NAME]` — apply NAME (or the default when no
153	  name) *to* the device.
154	- `default` means only "the profile bare `restore-state` uses" —
155	  **never** auto-applied. The tool stays passive by default;
156	  nothing writes to the device unless a `set-*`/`restore-state`
157	  verb asks. We think an auto-applied default would reintroduce
158	  the surprise-mutation path Issue #1 [[1]] warns about.
159	- The snapshot covers output config (reg 6 / 7 / 75). Baud
160	  (reg 5) is excluded from restore by default, because rewriting
161	  baud is itself the wedge trigger; `--baud` controls the link
162	  instead.
163	
164	### Open questions (resolve before coding the relevant step)
165	
166	- `set-bin` and `set-ascii` both present on one line: error
167	  ("pick one output to bench") vs measure both — leaning error.
168	- Persist: apply volatile by default (reverts on power cycle,
169	  matches today's bench), with `--persist` available.
170	- `reset` / `factory-reset` reboot the device mid-line — constrain
171	  them to standalone / last.
172	- Passive binary frame-rate needs a frame length: reuse
173	  `get-bin`'s reg-75 parser to self-configure, or sniff the
174	  binary sync byte `0xFA`. v1 may report byte throughput +
175	  ASCII line rate and add decoded binary rate via the reg-75 read.
176	
177	
178	## refactor: split main.rs into lib modules
179	
180	Commits: [[7]],[[8]]
181	
182	`main.rs` had grown to ~1890 lines, and the 0.3.0 bench redesign
183	adds a large new surface (the `get-*`/`set-*` verbs, the `+`-step
184	grammar, a TOML config, named states). We split the monolith into
185	a lib crate of focused modules first, so the new code lands in
186	clean modules instead of growing the single file. Each step is a
187	pure code-move — behavior unchanged, verified by the existing
188	tests + clippy + a smoke run.
189	
190	- `proto.rs` — VN-100 protocol primitives: `checksum` /
191	  `vn_crc16`, command framing (`build_command`,
192	  `verify_checksum`), `error_description`, the register parsers,
193	  and the `Field`/`FIELDS` binary-output vocabulary.
194	- `transact.rs` — port I/O: `read_reply`, `transact`,
195	  `transact_retry`, `send_reboot_command`.
196	- `cli.rs` — `parse_args`, the `Command` enum, and help text.
197	- `bench.rs` — the `bench` command (passive, built in 0.3.0-4).
198	- `lib.rs` ties the modules together; `main.rs` is thin — parse
199	  args, open the port, dispatch.
200	- Ladder: proto (-1), transact (-2), cli + bench scaffold (-3);
```

```
139	/// Format one help row: a `label` and one-or-more wrapped description lines,
140	/// aligned to a common description column. If the label is too wide, the
141	/// description starts on the next line.
142	fn help_row(label: &str, desc: &[&str]) -> String {
143	    const COL: usize = 24; // label column width; description starts after it
144	    let mut out = String::new();
145	    if label.len() <= COL {
146	        out.push_str(&format!("  {label:<COL$}{}\n", desc[0]));
147	    } else {
148	        out.push_str(&format!("  {label}\n"));
149	        out.push_str(&format!("  {:<COL$}{}\n", "", desc[0]));
150	    }
151	    for cont in &desc[1..] {
152	        out.push_str(&format!("  {:<COL$}{}\n", "", cont));
153	    }
154	    out
155	}
```

```
291	/// Parse CLI args into a connection config and a command.
292	pub fn parse_args<I: Iterator<Item = String>>(args: I) -> Result<(Config, Command), String> {
293	    let args: Vec<String> = args.collect();
294	    if args
295	        .iter()
296	        .any(|a| matches!(a.as_str(), "help" | "--help" | "-h"))
297	    {
298	        return Ok((
299	            Config {
300	                port: String::new(),
301	                baud: 0,
302	            },
303	            Command::Help,
304	        ));
305	    }
306	    if args
307	        .iter()
308	        .any(|a| matches!(a.as_str(), "--version" | "-V"))
309	    {
310	        return Ok((
311	            Config {
312	                port: String::new(),
313	                baud: 0,
314	            },
315	            Command::Version,
316	        ));
```

```
60	pub fn run() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
61	    let (config, command) = match parse_args(std::env::args().skip(1)) {
62	        Ok(parsed) => parsed,
63	        Err(e) => {
64	            eprintln!("error: {e}\n");
65	            eprint!("{}", help_text());
66	            std::process::exit(2);
67	        }
68	    };
69	
70	    if let Command::Help = command {
71	        print!("{}", help_text());
72	        return Ok(());
73	    }
74	
75	    if let Command::Version = command {
76	        println!("{}", version_line());
77	        return Ok(());
78	    }

80	    // Stamp a bench run with the tool version as its first line, so a captured

94	    // a fresh open at high baud often loses its first query.

96	    let _ = port.clear(serialport::ClearBuffer::Input);

101	         (VN-100 factory default is 115200; use --baud to match, or the `baud` command to change it)",
102	        config.baud
103	    );
104	
105	    match command {
106	        Command::Help | Command::Version => unreachable!("handled above"),
107	
108	        Command::GetHz => {

110	                &mut port,

118	        }

121	            let reply = transact_retry(
122	                &mut port,
123	                &build_command(&format!("VNWRG,07,{hz}")),
124	                5,
125	                |l| parse_reg07(l).is_some(),
126	                &no_reply,
127	            )?;
128	            println!("RX: {reply}");
129	            println!("Async output rate: {} Hz", parse_reg07(&reply).unwrap());
130	
131	            if persist {
132	                let confirm = transact_retry(
133	                    &mut port,
134	                    &build_command("VNWNV"),
135	                    5,
136	                    |l| l.starts_with("$VNWNV"),
137	                    &no_reply,
138	                )?;
139	                println!("RX: {confirm}");
140	                println!("Settings written to non-volatile memory.");
141	            }
142	        }
143	
144	        Command::SetBaud {
145	            baud: new_baud,
146	            persist,
147	        } => {
148	            // The device replies at the CURRENT baud, then switches to the new one.
149	            let reply = transact_retry(
150	                &mut port,
151	                &build_command(&format!("VNWRG,05,{new_baud}")),
152	                5,
153	                |l| l.starts_with("$VNWRG,05,"),
154	                &no_reply,
155	            )?;
156	            println!("RX: {reply}");
157	            println!("Device acknowledged baud change to {new_baud}.");
158
```

```
## Per-commit flow

Every commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes
through:

1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in
   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`.
2. **Do the work** (see [Iterative work](#iterative-work)
   for the loop-and-squash technique).
3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In
   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit.
4. **Validate the artifact** — a medium-specific step, skip-able
   for notes-only commits, mandatory at close-out. For the Rust
   example the cargo cycle is:
   1. `cargo fmt`
   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
   3. `cargo test`
   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)
5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;
   tell the user "ready to commit." The user reviews the
   changes and we iterate until complete.
6. **Write the commit description** — see
   [Commit description](#commit-description).
7. **Commit Description review.** Show the title + body
   and stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.
8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .` for
   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last
   keeps the verb visible):

   ```
   jj commit -m \
   "<type>: <short description>" \
   -m "<intro paragraph>

   - file1: gist
   - file2: gist

   ochid: /.claude/<chid>" \
   -R .
   ```

**Two overrides apply:**

- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates
  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and
  surface it; don't push through.
- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull
  a review or question forward.

## Commit description
```

```
## Step numbering

The cycle (Preparation -> Work -> Close-out; see
[cycle-protocol.md](cycle-protocol.md)) encodes each commit's
phase in the version suffix — the **final identifier `0` marks
a Preparation**:

- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation
- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits
- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)

**Preparation is optional.** A lightweight cycle — no ladder,
no setup commit — skips `-0` and starts at `-1` (its first
Work commit). The same holds at every level: a sub-cycle
needing no Preparation omits its `.0` (see Nesting). One that
grows a Preparation later adds the `0` step without
renumbering siblings.

Disambiguation:

- `-10` — Work commit #10 (final identifier `10`), not a
  Preparation.
- `-1.0` — Preparation of the `-1` sub-cycle (final
  identifier `0`).

**Nesting.** Sub-cycles append another level, recursively:

- `X.Y.Z-3.0` — Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle
- `X.Y.Z-3.1`, `X.Y.Z-3.2` — its Work
- `X.Y.Z-3` — its Close-out
- `X.Y.Z-3.1.0` — Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle

Bump the version-of-record at the start of each phase, so the
active phase is recorded — and, per the preference above,
every commit carries a distinct version.
```

```json
  "file_path": "/home/nps-gnc/data/prgs/nps-gnc/rw-vn100/src/bench.rs",
  "content": "//! The `bench` command — passively measure whatever the device is already\n//! streaming.\n//!\n//! - `bench` does **no** device writes: it reads the live serial stream for a\n//!   fixed window and reports what it sees. No configure, no restore.\n//! - The same bytes are scanned two ways at once:\n//!     - ASCII `$VN…` async lines (checksum-valid), and\n//!     - binary `0xFA` Common-group frames (CRC-valid).\n//!   plus a count of every byte off the wire for total throughput.\n//! - Passive means the binary frame length is unknown up front, so each frame\n//!   is sniffed from its own header (`try_common_frame`) rather than a config.\n\nuse std::io::{ErrorKind, Read};\nuse std::time::{Duration, Instant};\n\nuse crate::proto::*;\n\n// Binary frame: sync 0xFA, groups=0x01 (Common), one 16-bit field mask, the\n// streamed fields' payload, then a 2-byte CRC. Length depends on the field set.\nconst BENCH_SYNC: u8 = 0xFA;\nconst BENCH_GROUPS: u8 = 0x01;\nconst BENCH_HEADER: usize = 1 + 1 + 2; // sync + groups + field mask\nconst BENCH_CRC: usize = 2;\n\n/// What a passive bench run observed over its window.\n///\n/// - ASCII fields cover the `$VN…` async line stream.\n/// - Binary fields cover the `0xFA` Common-group frame stream; `bin_mask` and\n///   `bin_first` are from the first valid frame (the stream's field set is\n///   constant).\n/// - `total_bytes` is every byte read — both streams plus any line noise — for\n///   the wire-utilization figure.\nstruct Stats {\n    msgs: u64,\n    ascii_bytes: u64,\n    ascii_sample: Option<String>,\n    frames: u64,\n    bin_bytes: u64,\n    bin_mask: Option<u16>,\n    bin_first: Option<Vec<u8>>,\n    total_bytes: u64,\n    elapsed: f64,\n}\n\n/// Outcome of sniffing a possible binary frame at the start of a slice.\n///\n/// - `Frame(len)` — a CRC-valid Common frame of `len` bytes starts here.\n/// - `NeedMore` — a plausible frame prefix, but too short to decide yet.\n/// - `NotHere` — not a frame start (false sync, other group, or an unknown\n///   field bit we cannot size).\nenum Sniff {\n    Frame(usize),\n    NeedMore,\n    NotHere,\n}\n\n/// Read a little-endian f32 from `buf` at `off`.\nfn rd_f32(buf: &[u8], off: usize) -> f32 {\n    f32::from_le_bytes(buf[off..off + 4].try_into().unwrap()) // OK: caller slices a full frame\n}\n\n/// The known Common fields present in `mask`, in ascending bit order.\n///\n/// `FIELDS` is already bit-ordered, which is also the device's on-wire packing\n/// order — so the returned slice can drive `decode_binary_sample` directly.\nfn fields_from_mask(mask: u16) -> Vec<&'static Field> {\n    FIELDS\n        .iter()\n        .filter(|f| mask & (1u16 << f.bit) != 0)\n        .collect()\n}\n\n/// Sniff one Common-group binary frame at the start of `s`.\n///\n/// Passive: the frame length is read from the frame's own header — sync `0xFA`,\n/// groups byte `0x01` (Common), a 16-bit field mask — by summing the known\n/// fields' sizes for the payload, then the CRC confirms the guess.\nfn try_common_frame(s: &[u8]) -> Sniff {\n    if s.is_empty() || s[0] != BENCH_SYNC {\n        return Sniff::NotHere;\n    }\n    if s.len() < 2 {\n        return Sniff::NeedMore;\n    }\n    if s[1] != BENCH_GROUPS {\n        return Sniff::NotHere; // only the Common group is supported\n    }\n    if s.len() < BENCH_HEADER {\n        return Sniff::NeedMore;\n    }\n    let mask = u16::from_le_bytes([s[2], s[3]]);\n    if mask == 0 {\n        return Sniff::NotHere; // a real frame always carries at least one field\n    }\n    // Sum the payload from the known Common fields. A set bit we don't know\n    // can't be sized, so we can't find the frame end — treat it as a false sync.\n    let mut payload = 0usize;\n    let mut known = 0u16;\n    for f in FIELDS {\n        if mask & (1u16 << f.bit) != 0 {\n            payload += f.size;\n            known |= 1u16 << f.bit;\n        }\n    }\n    if known != mask {\n        return Sniff::NotHere;\n    }\n    let frame_len = BENCH_HEADER + payload + BENCH_CRC;\n    if s.len() < frame_len {\n        return Sniff::NeedMore;\n    }\n    // CRC runs over everything after the sync byte (groups..payload..crc); a\n    // correct frame makes it zero. 0xFA can appear in payload, so a CRC miss\n    // just means this sync was false.\n    if vn_crc16(&s[1..frame_len]) == 0 {\n        Sniff::Frame(frame_len)\n    } else {\n        Sniff::NotHere\n    }\n}\n\n/// Human-readable decode of the selected fields in one binary frame.\nfn decode_binary_sample(frame: &[u8], fields: &[&Field]) -> String {\n    let mut off = BENCH_HEADER;\n    let mut parts = Vec::new();\n    for f in fields {\n        let s = match f.name {\n            \"time\" => format!(\n                \"t={} ns\",\n                u64::from_le_bytes(frame[off..off + 8].try_into().unwrap()) // OK: field sized 8\n            ),\n            \"ypr\" => format!(\n                \"ypr=[{:.2}, {:.2}, {:.2}] deg\",\n                rd_f32(frame, off),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 4),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 8)\n            ),\n            \"quat\" => format!(\n                \"quat=[{:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}]\",\n                rd_f32(frame, off),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 4),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 8),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 12)\n            ),\n            \"gyro\" => format!(\n                \"gyro=[{:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}] rad/s\",\n                rd_f32(frame, off),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 4),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 8)\n            ),\n            \"accel\" => format!(\n                \"accel=[{:.3}, {:.3}, {:.3}] m/s^2\",\n                rd_f32(frame, off),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 4),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 8)\n            ),\n            \"imu\" => format!(\n                \"uncomp_accel=[{:.3}, {:.3}, {:.3}] m/s^2 uncomp_gyro=[{:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}] rad/s\",\n                rd_f32(frame, off),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 4),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 8),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 12),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 16),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 20)\n            ),\n            \"magpres\" => format!(\n                \"mag=[{:.3}, {:.3}, {:.3}] G temp={:.2} C pres={:.3} kPa\",\n                rd_f32(frame, off),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 4),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 8),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 12),\n                rd_f32(frame, off + 16)\n            ),\n            _ => \"?\".to_string(),\n        };\n        parts.push(s);\n        off += f.size;\n    }\n    parts.join(\", \")\n}\n\n/// True if `line` is an async data message (a `$VN…` line that isn't a command\n/// echo or error) with a valid checksum.\nfn is_async_line(line: &str) -> bool {\n    line.starts_with(\"$VN\")\n        && !line.starts_with(\"$VNRRG\")\n        && !line.starts_with(\"$VNWRG\")\n        && !line.starts_with(\"$VNERR\")\n        && verify_checksum(line).is_ok()\n}\n\n/// Passively read the live stream for `secs`, scanning the same bytes for ASCII\n/// async lines and binary Common frames at once.\n///\n/// - ASCII: split on `\\n`, count checksum-valid `$VN…` messages, keep the first\n///   as a sample.\n/// - Binary: accumulate bytes and pull out CRC-valid `0xFA` frames via\n///   `try_common_frame`, keeping the first frame for a decoded sample.\n/// - `total_bytes` counts every byte read, for the wire-utilization figure.\nfn measure<S: Read>(port: &mut S, secs: u64) -> std::io::Result<Stats> {\n    let start = Instant::now();\n    let deadline = start + Duration::from_secs(secs);\n    let mut buf = [0u8; 1024];\n\n    let mut total_bytes = 0u64;\n\n    // ASCII line-scanner state.\n    let mut line: Vec<u8> = Vec::new();\n    let mut msgs = 0u64;\n    let mut ascii_bytes = 0u64;\n    let mut ascii_sample: Option<String> = None;\n\n    // Binary frame-scanner state.\n    let mut acc: Vec<u8> = Vec::new();\n    let mut frames = 0u64;\n    let mut bin_bytes = 0u64;\n    let mut bin_mask: Option<u16> = None;\n    let mut bin_first: Option<Vec<u8>> = None;\n\n    while Instant::now() < deadline {\n        let n = match port.read(&mut buf) {\n            Ok(0) => continue,\n            Ok(n) => n,\n            Err(ref e) if matches!(e.kind(), ErrorKind::TimedOut | ErrorKind::WouldBlock) => {\n                continue;\n            }\n            Err(e) => return Err(e),\n        };\n        let chunk = &buf[..n];\n        total_bytes += n as u64;\n\n        // ASCII view: walk bytes, completing a line on each `\\n`.\n        for &b in chunk {\n            match b {\n                b'\\n' => {\n                    let raw = String::from_utf8_lossy(&line);\n                    let cand = match raw.rfind('$') {\n                        Some(p) => raw[p..].trim(),\n                        None => raw.trim(),\n                    };\n                    if is_async_line(cand) {\n                        msgs += 1;\n                        ascii_bytes += cand.len() as u64 + 2; // + CRLF\n                        if ascii_sample.is_none() {\n                            ascii_sample = Some(cand.to_string());\n                        }\n                    }\n                    line.clear();\n                }\n                b'\\r' => {}\n                _ => {\n                    line.push(b);\n                    if line.len() > 1024 {\n                        line.clear();\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Binary view: accumulate, then pull out CRC-valid Common frames.\n        acc.extend_from_slice(chunk);\n        let mut i = 0;\n        while i < acc.len() {\n            if acc[i] != BENCH_SYNC {\n                i += 1;\n                continue;\n            }\n            match try_common_frame(&acc[i..]) {\n                Sniff::Frame(flen) => {\n                    frames += 1;\n                    bin_bytes += flen as u64;\n                    if bin_mask.is_none() {\n                        bin_mask = Some(u16::from_le_bytes([acc[i + 2], acc[i + 3]]));\n                        bin_first = Some(acc[i..i + flen].to_vec());\n                    }\n                    i += flen;\n                }\n                Sniff::NotHere => i += 1,\n                Sniff::NeedMore => break, // partial frame at the tail; wait for more\n            }\n        }\n        acc.drain(0..i);\n        // Backstop: a stream with no valid frames never drains its tail prefix,\n        // so cap the buffer (keep enough to span any single frame).\n        if acc.len() > 8192 {\n            let cut = acc.len() - 512;\n            acc.drain(0..cut);\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(Stats {\n        msgs,\n        ascii_bytes,\n        ascii_sample,\n        frames,\n        bin_bytes,\n        bin_mask,\n        bin_first,\n        total_bytes,\n        elapsed: start.elapsed().as_secs_f64(),\n    })\n}\n\n/// Print the passive bench result: ASCII rate, binary rate, wire throughput.\nfn report(baud: u32, st: &Stats) {\n    let secs = st.elapsed;\n    let rate = |count: u64| if secs > 0.0 { count as f64 / secs } else { 0.0 };\n    println!();\n\n    if st.msgs > 0 {\n        println!(\n            \"ASCII:  {} messages in {secs:.2}s = {:.1} Hz ({} B/s).\",\n            st.msgs,\n            rate(st.msgs),\n            (st.ascii_bytes as f64 / secs).round() as u64\n        );\n        if let Some(s) = &st.ascii_sample {\n            println!(\"  Sample: {s}\");\n        }\n    } else {\n        println!(\"ASCII:  none seen.\");\n    }\n\n    if st.frames > 0 {\n        let mask = st.bin_mask.unwrap_or(0); // OK: frames>0 implies a mask was captured\n        let fields = fields_from_mask(mask);\n        let names: Vec<&str> = fields.iter().map(|f| f.name).collect();\n        let flen = st.bin_first.as_ref().map(|f| f.len()).unwrap_or(0); // OK: frames>0 implies a frame\n        println!(\n            \"Binary: {} frames in {secs:.2}s = {:.1} Hz ({} B/s).\",\n            st.frames,\n            rate(st.frames),\n            (st.bin_bytes as f64 / secs).round() as u64\n        );\n        println!(\"  Fields: Common{names:?} ({flen} B/frame).\");\n        if let Some(fr) = &st.bin_first {\n            println!(\"  Sample: {}\", decode_binary_sample(fr, &fields));\n        }\n    } else {\n        println!(\"Binary: none seen.\");\n    }\n\n    let bits = if secs > 0.0 {\n        st.total_bytes as f64 * 10.0 / secs\n    } else {\n        0.0\n    };\n    let pct = 100.0 * bits / baud as f64;\n    println!(\n        \"Wire throughput ~{:.0} kbit/s = {:.0}% of the {:.1} kbit/s {baud}-baud link.\",\n        bits / 1000.0,\n        pct,\n        baud as f64 / 1000.0\n    );\n}\n\n/// Passively measure the device's live output for `secs` seconds.\n///\n/// - Does no device writes — reports whatever is already streaming.\n/// - Counts ASCII `$VN` async lines and binary `0xFA` Common-group frames\n///   (CRC-checked) over the same bytes, plus total wire throughput against the\n///   `baud` link.\npub fn bench<S: Read>(\n    port: &mut S,\n    baud: u32,\n    secs: u64,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    println!(\"Measuring the live stream for {secs}s (passive — no device writes)...\");\n    let st = measure(port, secs)?;\n    report(baud, &st);\n    if st.msgs == 0 && st.frames == 0 {\n        return Err(\n            \"saw no ASCII messages or binary frames — is the device streaming, \\\n             and does --baud match its current rate?\"\n                .into(),\n        );\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n"
}
```

```
1	//! Command-line surface — argument parsing, the `Command` enum, and help.
2	//!
3	//! - `parse_args` turns argv into a `Config` (port/baud) plus a `Command`.
4	//! - `help_text` renders usage; the ASCII-type and serial-port helpers map
5	//!   CLI names to register values.
6	
7	use crate::proto::*;
8	
9	/// Frequencies (Hz) the VN-100 accepts for the async data output rate.
10	/// Authoritative: REFERENCE.md "Register 7" (ICD §3.2.4 Table 3.9; vnsdk AsyncOutputFreq::Adof).
11	const VALID_RATES: &[u32] = &[1, 2, 4, 5, 10, 20, 25, 40, 50, 100, 200];
12	
13	/// Serial baud rates the VN-100 supports (register 5).
14	/// Authoritative: REFERENCE.md "Register 5" (ICD §3.2.2 Table 3.3; vnsdk BaudRate::BaudRates).
15	const VALID_BAUDS: &[u32] = &[
16	    9600, 19200, 38400, 57600, 115200, 128000, 230400, 460800, 921600,
17	];
18	
19	/// ASCII async message presets (register 6 ADOR): CLI name -> register value.
20	/// Authoritative: REFERENCE.md "Register 6" (ICD §3.2.3 Table 3.6;
21	/// vnsdk AsyncOutputType::Ador).
22	const ASCII_TYPES: &[(&str, u8)] = &[
23	    ("off", 0),
24	    ("ypr", 1),
25	    ("qtn", 2),
26	    ("qmr", 8),
27	    ("mag", 10),
28	    ("acc", 11),
29	    ("gyr", 12),
30	    ("mar", 13),
31	    ("ymr", 14), // the factory default
32	    ("yba", 16),
33	    ("yia", 17),
34	    ("imu", 19),
35	    ("dtv", 30),
36	    ("hve", 34),
37	];
38	
39	fn ascii_type_names() -> String {
40	    ASCII_TYPES
41	        .iter()
42	        .map(|(n, _)| *n)
43	        .collect::<Vec<_>>()
44	        .join(", ")
45	}
46	
47	/// Resolve a `--type` name (case-insensitive, optional `vn` prefix) to its ADOR.
48	fn parse_ascii_type(s: &str) -> Result<u8, String> {
49	    let lower = s.trim().to_lowercase();
50	    let key = lower.strip_prefix("vn").unwrap_or(&lower);
51	    ASCII_TYPES
52	        .iter()
53	        .find(|(n, _)| *n == key)
54	        .map(|(_, v)| *v)
55	        .ok_or_else(|| {
56	            format!(
57	                "unknown ASCII type `{s}`; choose from {}",
58	                ascii_type_names()
59	            )
60	        })
61	}
62	
63	/// Parse the `--serial-port` value into a register-75 `asyncMode` code.
64	///
65	/// - `1` / `2` target one of the VN-100's two UARTs; `both` (or `3`) targets
66	///   both. The default elsewhere is `2`, the RPi5 TTL header (the flight
67	///   target); the RS-232 bench is port 1. Selecting a port also subjects it to
68	///   the register-75 fit check, so `both` is avoided as a default — a port left
69	///   at a low baud would veto a frame the connected port could take.
70	fn parse_serial_port(s: &str) -> Result<u8, String> {
71	    match s.trim().to_lowercase().as_str() {
72	        "1" => Ok(1),
73	        "2" => Ok(2),
74	        "3" | "both" => Ok(3),
75	        other => Err(format!(
76	            "invalid --serial-port `{other}`; choose 1, 2, or both"
77	        )),
78	    }
79	}
80	
81	/// Display name for an ADOR value, e.g. 8 -> "VNYMR".
82	pub fn ascii_type_name(value: u8) -> String {
83	    ASCII_TYPES
84	        .iter()
85	        .find(|(_, v)| *v == value)
86	        .map(|(n, _)| {
87	            if *n == "off" {
88	                "off".to_string()
89	            } else {
90	                format!("VN{}", n.to_uppercase())
91	            }
92	        })
93	        .unwrap_or_else(|| format!("ADOR {value}"))
94	}
95	
```

CLI names to register values.\n\nuse crate::proto::*;\n\n/// Frequencies (Hz) the VN-100 accepts for the async data output rate.\n/// Authoritative: REFERENCE.md \"Register 7\" (ICD §3.2.4 Table 3.9; vnsdk AsyncOutputFreq::Adof).\nconst VALID_RATES: &[u32] = &[1, 2, 4, 5, 10, 20, 25, 40, 50, 100, 200];\n\n/// Serial baud rates the VN-100 supports (register 5).\n/// Authoritative: REFERENCE.md \"Register 5\" (ICD §3.2.2 Table 3.3; vnsdk BaudRate::BaudRates).\nconst VALID_BAUDS: &[u32] = &[\n    9600, 19200, 38400, 57600, 115200, 128000, 230400, 460800, 921600,\n];\n\n/// ASCII async message presets (register 6 ADOR): CLI name -> register value.\n/// Authoritative: REFERENCE.md \"Register 6\" (ICD §3.2.3 Table 3.6;\n/// vnsdk AsyncOutputType::Ador).\nconst ASCII_TYPES: &[(&str, u8)] = &[\n    (\"off\", 0),\n    (\"ypr\", 1),\n    (\"qtn\", 2),\n    (\"qmr\", 8),\n    (\"mag\", 10),\n    (\"acc\", 11),\n    (\"gyr\", 12),\n    (\"mar\", 13),\n    (\"ymr\", 14), // the factory default\n    (\"yba\", 16),\n    (\"yia\", 17),\n    (\"imu\", 19),\n    (\"dtv\", 30),\n    (\"hve\", 34),\n];\n\nfn ascii_type_names() -> String {\n    ASCII_TYPES\n        .iter()\n        .map(|(n, _)| *n)\n        .collect::<Vec<_>>()\n        .join(\", \")\n}\n\n/// Resolve a `--type` name (case-insensitive, optional `vn` prefix) to its ADOR.\nfn parse_ascii_type(s: &str) -> Result<u8, String> {\n    let lower = s.trim().to_lowercase();\n    let key = lower.strip_prefix(\"vn\").unwrap_or(&lower);\n    ASCII_TYPES\n        .iter()\n        .find(|(n, _)| *n == key)\n        .map(|(_, v)| *v)\n        .ok_or_else(|| {\n            format!(\n                \"unknown ASCII type `{s}`; choose from {}\",\n                ascii_type_names()\n            )\n        })\n}\n\n/// Parse the `--serial-port` value into a register-75 `asyncMode` code.\n///\n/// - `1` / `2` target one of the VN-100's two UARTs; `both` (or `3`) targets\n///   both. The default elsewhere is `2`, the RPi5 TTL header (the flight\n///   target); the RS-232 bench is port 1. Selecting a port also subjects it to\n///   the register-75 fit check, so `both` is avoided as a default — a port left\n///   at a low baud would veto a frame the connected port could take.\nfn parse_serial_port(s: &str) -> Result<u8, String> {\n    match s.trim().to_lowercase().as_str() {\n        \"1\" => Ok(1),\n        \"2\" => Ok(2),\n        \"3\" | \"both\" => Ok(3),\n        other => Err(format!(\n            \"invalid --serial-port `{other}`; choose 1, 2, or both\"\n        )),\n    }\n}\n\n/// Display name for an ADOR value, e.g. 8 -> \"VNYMR\".\npub fn ascii_type_name(value: u8) -> String {\n    ASCII_TYPES\n        .iter()\n        .find(|(_, v)| *v == value)\n        .map(|(n, _)| {\n            if *n == \"off\" {\n                \"off\".to_string()\n            } else {\n                format!(\"VN{}\", n.to_uppercase())\n            }\n        })\n        .unwrap_or_else(|| format!(\"ADOR {value}\"))\n}\n",

Hmm, how are we going to set the "--fields" for binary and "--type" for ascii and why two different parameters why not just use --fields but list/validate the two different valid sets for bin/ascii?

> AGENT

Good — let me stop the deletion and answer this first, because it's really a **-5 design question** (the config verbs), and it's worth settling before I tear out the parsers that feed it.

## Why they're two different things today

The asymmetry isn't an arbitrary tool choice — it's forced by the VN‑100 ICD. The two outputs are controlled by two registers with fundamentally different *shapes*:

- **ASCII async = register 6 (ADOR), a pick‑*one* enum.** You choose a single named preset message. That's the `ASCII_TYPES` table I was about to delete — `ypr`, `qtn`, `ymr`, `imu`, … each is one `u8`, a whole canned message format. You cannot compose them; `VNYMR` is a *fixed bundle* (ypr+mag+accel+gyro). There is no "ASCII with just time+accel" mode in the device.
- **Binary = register 75, a pick‑*many* bitmask.** That's `FIELDS` — `time`, `ypr`, `quat`, `gyro`, `accel`, `imu`, `magpres`, each an independent bit. `--fields time,accel,gyro` is a composed subset (the mask we sniff in passive bench).

So one is **"select a preset" (enum)** and the other is **"compose a field set" (bitmask)**. Different verbs, different grammars, *disjoint vocabularies* (an ASCII preset name like `ymr` is not a binary field; a binary field like `magpres` is not an ASCII preset).

## Why a single `--fields` for both is worse, not better

A unified `--fields` that "validates against the right set per mode" would be **one flag hiding two grammars**:
- In binary mode it's a comma‑*list* of composable atoms.
- In ASCII mode it would have to be exactly *one* name from a different list — i.e. it's `--type` wearing `--fields`' clothes.

The flag's meaning would silently flip on the `--bin` mode switch, and the help could never show one coherent "Choices:" line. That's more surprising than two honestly‑named parameters.

## But — in the new design this mostly dissolves

In the passive‑bench redesign (chores‑01), neither is a bench flag anymore. They become **two verbs whose names encode the register**:
- `set-ascii=<preset|off>` → reg 6
- `set-bin=<fields|off>` → reg 75

The verb name *is* the disambiguator, so "why two parameters" answers itself — they're two different operations on two different registers. A single `set-output=…` would just reintroduce the same hidden mode‑switch problem.

---

So my recommendation is to keep the `set-ascii` / `set-bin` split as chores‑01 specifies. Given that, deleting `parse_ascii_type` / `parse_serial_port` in **-4** is still right — they'll be rewritten fresh as part of the `set-*` verbs in -5 regardless of which way we go.

Two things for you to decide before I continue:

1. **Keep the planned `set-ascii` / `set-bin` two-verb split?** (my rec: yes) — or do you want to explore a unified `set-output`/`--fields` after all?
2. For **-4 right now**: OK to delete those two parsers (they return in -5), or would you rather I leave them parked in place until -5 needs them?

> AGENT

Yes — you've got it, and you've actually landed on exactly why passive bench is nicer than the old one.

**The two registers are independent and you configure them separately:**
- `set-ascii=<preset>` → reg 6 (ASCII async on/off + which preset)
- `set-bin=<fields>` → reg 75 (binary on/off + which fields)

(both arriving in -5). You set/initialize whichever you want — one, the other, or both — *then* bench.

**But bench itself needs no `--bin`/`--ascii` selector — that's the whole point of -4.** The new passive `bench` doesn't *configure* anything, so it has nothing to select. It just reads the wire and reports whatever is actually flowing, labeled:

```
ASCII:  200 messages in 5.00s = 40.0 Hz (...).
  Sample: $VNYMR,...
Binary: 1000 frames in 5.00s = 199.9 Hz (...).
  Fields: Common[time, ypr, ...] (110 B/frame).
  Sample: t=... ns, ...
Wire throughput ~220 kbit/s = 24% of the 921.6 kbit/s link.
```

If only binary is on, the ASCII line reads `ASCII:  none seen.`, and vice‑versa. So **"we'll see the appropriate items that run" happens automatically** — no flag required. Adding `--ascii`/`--bin` back to bench would re‑couple it to a mode it no longer needs to know about, which is exactly the coupling -4 removes.

A couple of nuances worth flagging:

- **Both can be live at once.** You can have ASCII async *and* binary streaming together, and bench will report both rates. (Caveat: they share the port's baud budget — the device's combined-load fit check means the *sum* has to fit the link. That's a `set-*` concern, not a bench concern.)
- Once the **step grammar** lands (-6), the configure‑then‑measure flow collapses to one line over one connection, e.g.:
  ```
  set-bin=time,accel,gyro+set-hz=200 bench
  ```
  but until then you'd just run `set-bin …` then `bench` as separate invocations.

So the design holds: two `set-*` verbs to configure, one mode‑agnostic passive `bench` to observe. Where I'd paused — deleting `parse_ascii_type` / `parse_serial_port` — is still the right move; they get rewritten as the `set-*` verbs in -5.

Don't apologize — this is exactly the right time to raise it, before anything's committed. Having thought about it, **I think you're right, and I'd lean toward the swap.** Here's the honest reasoning.

## Why set-ascii / set-bin first is the better order

It's the **expand-then-contract** principle: build the replacement before removing the thing it replaces.

- **Today's working tool configures via `bench --bin --fields …`.** Passive bench (-4) *removes* that ability. But the replacement (`set-bin`/`set-ascii`) doesn't land until -5. So the current order has a one-step **capability gap**: between -4 and -5 the only way to turn binary on is raw `wrg 75 …`. Doing the verbs first means the tool is never less capable than it is right now.
- **Testability cuts the same way.** Passive bench is *hard to exercise* without a convenient way to turn binary on. Right after your 921600 run the device is back to ASCII-only @ 40 Hz (old bench restores), so a passive `bench` today would show binary "none seen." With `set-bin=time,accel,gyro` in hand first, validating passive bench is trivial: `set-bin …` then `bench`. Without it, I'm testing -4 through raw `wrg` or a hand-staged device state.

So: **verbs first gives you a continuously-capable tool *and* makes passive bench easy to validate when its turn comes.**

## The one wrinkle

The binary **rate** lives in reg 75 as a divisor (`800/N`), same register as the field mask. The clean "`set-bin=…+set-hz=N` → one combined write" depends on the `+`-join step grammar (-6). Before -6, `set-bin` can still land — it just writes the mask at the device's *current* divisor, and setting the binary rate standalone needs a read-modify-write of reg 75 (or you fall back to `wrg 75`). Workable, slightly less elegant. It doesn't block the swap; it just means -5's `set-hz`-for-binary story is a touch rougher until -6 tightens it.

## Proposed reordered ladder

- `0.3.0-4` **decompose config verbs** — `get/set-ascii` (reg 6), `get/set-bin` (reg 75). Keep the *old* bench as-is for now (parallel path).
- `0.3.0-5` **passive bench** — now safe to strip bench's configure/measure/restore, since the verbs replace it.
- `0.3.0-6` step grammar + one connection (incl. the merged reg-75 write).
- `0.3.0-7` named states.

## Cost of the swap right now

The in-progress -4 edits (Cargo.toml bump, the `bench.rs` passive rewrite, lib.rs dispatch, the partial cli.rs gutting) become premature. Nothing's committed, so reverting the working copy loses nothing permanent — and the passive-bench code is simple to reproduce when -5's turn comes. The tree is currently mid-surgery and doesn't compile anyway, so it needs a reset regardless.

**My recommendation: swap to verbs-first, revert the working copy to clean `main`, and start the new -4 (config verbs).** Want me to do that? If you'd rather keep passive-bench-first after all, say so and I'll just finish the -4 I started.

Done — ladder swapped, `(current)` now on the new **-4 (config verbs)**, and the working copy is back to clean `main` (the old configure/measure `bench` is intact as the parallel path). The `-5` entry now notes it's exercisable via the `-4` verbs.

Before I write any code for the new -4, the config verbs have a few genuinely-open design points (chores-01 even flags some as "resolve before coding"). Here's my proposed shape — mostly settled by the design note, with two real forks for you.

## New -4: `get/set-ascii` (reg 6), `get/set-bin` (reg 75)

Settled by chores-01, my recommended semantics:
- `get-ascii` → read reg 6, print the preset (e.g. `VNYMR`).
- `set-ascii <preset|off>` → reg 6 ADOR; `off` writes 0.
- `get-bin` → read reg 75, print port / divisor / fields.
- `set-bin <fields|off>` → read-modify-write reg 75: `off` sets asyncMode 0; a field list sets the mask (groups=01) on port 2 (the RPi5 default), **preserving the current divisor**.
- **Persist**: volatile by default, `--persist` to write flash — same as today's `set-hz`/`baud`.

## Two forks I shouldn't guess on

**1. Argument syntax now, or the eventual `=`/`+` form?**
The chores design's final grammar is `set-bin=time,accel` (so `+`-joins compose in -6). But -4 lands before the step grammar.
- *(Rec)* **Positional now** — `set-bin time,accel`, matching today's `set-hz 40`. Migrate to `=`/`+` in -6. Keeps -4 small; the `=` form only earns its keep once `+`-joins exist.
- **Introduce `=` now** — `set-bin=time,accel` from the start, avoiding a later surface change.

**2. Is `set-hz` universal in -4, or still ASCII-only until -6?**
The binary rate is a divisor *inside* reg 75, so a universal `set-hz` for binary wants the merged reg-75 write that the `+`-join gives you in -6.
- *(Rec)* **ASCII-only in -4** (`set-hz` = reg 7, as today). `set-bin` keeps the current binary divisor; changing the binary *rate* waits for -6's `set-bin+set-hz` merge (or raw `wrg 75` meanwhile). This is the cleaner seam — but it means a fresh device can't hit 200 Hz *binary* through the verbs alone until -6.
- **Make `set-hz` universal now** — `set-hz 200` does a reg-75 read-modify-write when binary is active. More -4 code, pulls a chunk of -6 forward.

There's also one wrinkle to note (not a fork): reg 6 conflates ASCII on/off with the preset (0 = off), so there's no separate "enable" bit — I'd make **bare `set-ascii` an error** (nothing to enable without a preset), unlike bare `set-bin` which can re-enable a known mask. Shout if you'd rather it default to a preset.

```
85	- **Passive bench.** `bench [SECS]` measures whatever is already
86	  streaming — no device writes, no restore — default 5 s. This
87	  removes the configure→measure→restore dance that is bench's
88	  current bulk and its only wedge surface.
89	- **Decomposed config verbs**, each owning one register concern:
90	    - `get-hz` / `set-hz=<HZ>` — reg 7 async rate (exists today).
91	    - `get-ascii` / `set-ascii=<MODE|off>` — reg 6 ASCII preset.
92	    - `get-bin` / `set-bin=<FIELDS|off>` — reg 75 binary output.
93	    - Bare `set-bin` enables binary with the *current* fields;
94	      bare `set-ascii` enables ASCII with the current preset.
95	- **One connection per invocation** — the whole line opens the
96	  port once and runs its steps in order. See
97	  [Step grammar](#step-grammar-joined-tokens-space-separated-steps).
98	  Reading a register while that one connection is already streaming
99	  — and why rw-vn100 may discard stream data when it does — is in
100	  ARCHITECTURE.md [[9]].
101	- **Universal rate.** `set-hz=N` is a desired rate independent of
102	  output mode. See
103	  [Universal rate (option A)](#universal-rate-option-a).
104	- **File-backed named states.** A TOML config holds named
105	  profiles. See [Named states](#named-states-file-backed--default).
106	
107	### Step grammar: +-joined tokens, space-separated steps
108	
109	The CLI line is one open connection and a sequence of steps. The
110	grammar rides on shell word-splitting so there is no custom
111	space handling.
112	
113	- Each shell *word* is one **step**; steps execute left-to-right.
114	- Within a word, tokens joined by `+` apply together as one
115	  atomic unit; multiple `set-*` in the same word merge into a
116	  single device write.
117	- Comma stays reserved for value-internal lists
118	  (`set-bin=time,accel`); `+` is the only token joiner — never
119	  comma — so the grammar stays unambiguous and flat (the
120	  comma-nested alternative would make a value contain
121	  sub-assignments, i.e. recursion).
122	- Intra-step order is also left-to-right: `set-hz=50+bench` =
123	  apply, then measure.
124	- This unlocks a single-connection **sweep**:
125	  `save-state set-hz=50+bench set-hz=100+bench set-hz=200+bench
126	  restore-state` — snapshot, three configure-and-measure steps,
127	  restore, all on one open.
128	
129	### Universal rate (option A)
130	
131	`set-hz=N` reads as one desired rate regardless of mode; the
132	tool maps it to the right register.
133	
134	- ASCII: writes reg 7 directly.
135	- Binary: the rate is the rateDivisor *inside* reg 75 (`800/N`),
136	  the same register as the field mask — so `set-bin=…+set-hz=N`
137	  must resolve to one combined reg-75 write. The `+`-join is what
138	  makes that explicit: same word → one write.
139	- Bare `set-bin` with no `set-hz` in the same step keeps the
140	  device's current divisor.
141	
142	### Named states (file-backed) + default
143	
144	The `--config` file (default `./.rw-vn100-config.toml`, TOML,
145	hand-editable) holds a map of named profiles plus a `default`
146	key. Three verbs, three distinct jobs:
147	
148	- `save-state[=NAME]` — capture the current *device* regs into
149	  NAME (or the default name).
150	- `set-state=NAME+set-…` — define NAME from *explicit* values,
151	  no device read.
152	- `restore-state[=NAME]` — apply NAME (or the default when no
153	  name) *to* the device.
154	- `default` means only "the profile bare `restore-state` uses" —
155	  **never** auto-applied. The tool stays passive by default;
156	  nothing writes to the device unless a `set-*`/`restore-state`
157	  verb asks. We think an auto-applied default would reintroduce
158	  the surprise-mutation path Issue #1 [[1]] warns about.
159	- The snapshot covers output config (reg 6 / 7 / 75). Baud
160	  (reg 5) is excluded from restore by default, because rewriting
161	  baud is itself the wedge trigger; `--baud` controls the link
162	  instead.
163	
164	### Open questions (resolve before coding the relevant step)
```

```
238	
239	### VN-100 register-75 serial-port numbering on TTL
240	
241	The VN-100 binary-output register (75) names a target serial port
242	in its first field (`asyncMode`): 1, 2, or 3 (both). ASCII async
243	(registers 6/7) instead targets whichever port the config command
244	arrived on, so it needs no port number and "just works" on the
245	connected wire — which is why `get-hz` and the ASCII `bench`
246	succeeded immediately. `rw-vn100` hardcodes `asyncMode=1`
247	(`main.rs:1060`, `bench_binary`), which on the Pi's wire emits no
248	binary at all.
249	
250	Measured on `/dev/ttyAMA0`, identical field set (time+accel,
251	200 Hz), 2 s raw capture via `../fc/scripts/serial_check.py read`:
252	
253	- `asyncMode=1` → 9760 B, ASCII `$VNYMR` only, no `0xFA` frames.
254	- `asyncMode=2` → 20145 B, `0xFA` binary frames present.
255	- `asyncMode=3` → 20151 B, `0xFA` binary frames present.
256	
257	So the Pi's serial port (its one TX/RX pair on GPIO14/15) is the
258	VN-100's serial port **2** in the register-75 numbering; the bench
259	RS-232 rig used port 1.
260	
261	Port 1 and port 2 are two *separate physical UART interfaces* on
262	the VN-100 — different pin sets — not two logical streams sharing
263	one line. We are wired to exactly one of them (port 2); port 1 is
264	the interface the bench RS-232 transceiver used, and the Pi is not
265	connected to it at all. What *is* interleaved on our one port is
266	two message *types*, not two ports:
267
```

```
## fix: bench silences async before binary config

Commits: [[5]]

`bench --bin` could reject a binary config with `$VNERR` 0x0C
("insufficient baud rate") at a low baud even when the binary stream
fits the link with room to spare. The cause was write ordering:
bench configured the binary output (reg 75) while ASCII async (reg
7) was still streaming, so the device's fit check saw the *combined*
ASCII + binary load. The fix silences ASCII async before writing the
binary config, so the check sees only the binary load.

### Bench combined-load fit check at low baud

Observed 2026-06-26 on the RPi5 TTL header (`/dev/ttyAMA0`) running
`bench --bin` after an in-session baud switch to 57600:

- At 57600 with ASCII async still at 40 Hz, `VNWRG,75,2,20,01,0101`
  (time+accel, 26 B/frame, 40 Hz) was rejected with `$VNERR` 0x0C.
- The binary stream alone is ~10 kbit/s — 18% of the 57600 link —
  so it fits easily; only the transient ASCII-40 + binary-40
  combination overran the check.
- Lowering ASCII async to 4 Hz first, then re-running the identical
  bench, the same `VNWRG,75` was accepted and streamed clean at
  39.9 Hz. Only the ASCII rate changed, isolating the combined load
  as the cause.

We think the device enforces `message_size × rate ≤ baud` on every
reg-5 / reg-7 / reg-75 write, against the *sum* of the streams
configured on that port. At 115200 the headroom hid the transient;
at 57600 it surfaced. The ordering was always latent and only became
visible at the lower baud.

The fix reorders `bench_binary`:

- Before: read reg 7 → write reg 75 → write reg 7=0. The fit check
  fired on the reg-75 write, before ASCII was silenced.
- After: read reg 7 → write reg 7=0 → write reg 75, and restore reg
  7 if the reg-75 write still fails — so a genuinely-too-big binary
  frame (e.g. the 38 B frame at 200 Hz / 115200, [[2]]) no longer
  leaves ASCII async switched off.
- `bench_ascii` is unaffected: it has a single stream and already
  writes the message type (reg 6) before the rate (reg 7).

## Rename rdwr_vn100 to rw-vn100
```

> AGENT

Yes — and it's confirmed by measurement, not just the spec. Two independent pieces of evidence in chores-01:

**1. They interleave on one physical port.** ASCII async (regs 6/7) and binary (reg 75) are *message types*, not separate ports. From the port-numbering investigation:

> What *is* interleaved on our one port is two message *types*, not two ports

ASCII async targets whichever port the command arrived on; binary's `asyncMode` names a port. Point both at the same UART (our port 2 on `/dev/ttyAMA0`) and `$VN…` lines and `0xFA` frames come out the same wire, interleaved.

**2. The device itself treats them as a combined load on that port.** This is the strongest proof — measured 2026-06-26 at 57600 baud:

- ASCII async at 40 Hz **plus** a binary 40 Hz config → device rejected the binary write with `$VNERR 0x0C` because the fit check saw the **sum** of both streams.
- Drop ASCII to 4 Hz, re-run the identical binary write → accepted, streamed clean at 39.9 Hz.

If they couldn't coexist on one port there'd be no "sum" to overflow. We think the device enforces `message_size × rate ≤ baud` against the *total* of every stream configured on that port.

So: **yes, both at once on the same port, sharing the baud budget.** This is actually why the passive `bench` I'd drafted scans for ASCII lines *and* binary frames over the same bytes and reports both — that design is the right shape precisely because both can be live.

One design consequence worth flagging for -4, though — it's the flip side of your question: when `set-bin` writes reg 75 while ASCII async is *still streaming*, that same combined fit-check can reject it at low baud even though binary alone fits. The old `bench` dodged this by silencing ASCII first. But a standalone `set-bin` verb silently turning ASCII off would be a surprise mutation (exactly the passivity we're protecting). So we'll need to decide how `set-bin` handles a `$VNERR 0x0C`: surface it with a hint ("ASCII async is consuming the budget — lower it or turn it off first") rather than auto-silencing. Want me to fold that into the -4 design now, or park it as an open question until we hit it?

> AGENT

Parked — it's now an open question in chores-01, with the lean recorded (surface the `0x0C` with a hint, don't auto-silence ASCII). The design note is internally consistent again (subsection renamed, anchor fixed, `set-hz` examples updated).

On your question — **yes, your understanding is correct: you pick one stream, you don't need both.** But let me sharpen the "either can deliver" part, because it's not quite symmetric:

- **Binary (reg 75) — the flexible one.** You compose an *arbitrary* field set via the bitmask (`time`, `ypr`, `quat`, `gyro`, `accel`, `imu`, `magpres`, …), at full `f32` precision, in a compact frame. It can deliver essentially *any* data set you ask for. This is why fc.py runs binary at 200 Hz — it's the only one that's both complete and efficient enough.
- **ASCII (reg 6) — fixed bundles.** You pick *one preset* from a menu (`VNYMR`, `VNQMR`, `VNIMU`, …). Each is a canned bundle — you can't compose. The numbers are rounded text, and it's ~2× the bytes on the wire.

So the asymmetry: **binary can always deliver "all the data" you want; ASCII can deliver it only if a preset happens to match your desired fields.** "Either can deliver that" holds for the common cases a preset covers (ypr/mag/accel/gyro), but binary is strictly more capable.

Running **both at once is purely redundant for data delivery** — same information twice, doubling the wire load against that shared baud budget. The *only* reason you'd ever do it is a niche convenience: a low-rate human-readable ASCII feed for eyeballing, alongside the compact binary feed your code consumes. That's a monitoring choice, never a data-completeness need.

So for the flight controller: one stream, binary. The two `set-*` verbs being independent just means you *can* have both live — not that you'd want to.

Where to next — ready to move from design into coding the -4 verbs, or more to talk through first?

```
90	/// Compute the VN-100 checksum: XOR of all bytes in `payload`.
91	pub fn checksum(payload: &str) -> u8 {
92	    payload.bytes().fold(0u8, |acc, b| acc ^ b)
93	}
94	
95	/// Build a full command line (including `$`, `*`, checksum and CRLF) from the
96	/// payload that sits between `$` and `*`, e.g. `"VNRRG,07"`.
97	pub fn build_command(payload: &str) -> String {
98	    format!("${}*{:02X}\r\n", payload, checksum(payload))
99	}
100	
101	/// Verify the trailing `*XX` checksum of a received `$...*XX` line.
102	pub fn verify_checksum(line: &str) -> Result<(), String> {
103	    let payload = line.strip_prefix('$').ok_or("reply missing leading '$'")?;
104	    let (payload, sum) = payload
105	        .rsplit_once('*')
106	        .ok_or("reply missing '*' checksum delimiter")?;
107	    let given = u8::from_str_radix(sum.trim(), 16)
108	        .map_err(|_| format!("malformed checksum field {sum:?}"))?;
109	    let actual = checksum(payload);
110	    if given == actual {
111	        Ok(())
112	    } else {
113	        Err(format!(
114	            "checksum mismatch: reply says {given:02X}, computed {actual:02X}"
115	        ))
116	    }
117	}
118	
119	/// Human-readable description of a VN-100 system error code.
120	/// Authoritative: REFERENCE.md "Error responses" (ICD §1.5 Table 1.6; vnsdk Errors.hpp Error).
121	pub fn error_description(code: u8) -> &'static str {
122	    match code {
123	        1 => "hard fault",
124	        2 => "serial buffer overflow",
125	        3 => "invalid checksum",
126	        4 => "invalid command",
127	        5 => "not enough parameters",
128	        6 => "too many parameters",
129	        7 => "invalid parameter",
130	        8 => "invalid register",
131	        9 => "unauthorized access",
132	        10 => "watchdog reset",
133	        11 => "output buffer overflow",
134	        12 => "insufficient baud rate",
135	        255 => "error buffer overflow",
136	        _ => "unknown error",
137	    }
138	}
139	
140	/// Turn a `$VNERR,<code>*XX` line into a readable message. The code is hex.
141	pub fn vnerr_message(line: &str) -> String {
142	    let code = line
143	        .strip_prefix("$VNERR,")
144	        .and_then(|b| b.split('*').next())
145	        .map(str::trim)
146	        .and_then(|c| u8::from_str_radix(c, 16).ok());
147	    match code {
148	        Some(n) => {
149	            let mut msg = format!("device error 0x{n:02X} ({n}): {}", error_description(n));
150	            if n == 12 {
151	                // By far the most likely error when setting a high output rate.
152	                msg.push_str(
153	                    " — the configured async message won't fit at this output rate over the \
154	                     current serial baud; raise the baud (e.g. `baud 921600`) or shorten the \
155	                     async message (register 6)",
156	                );
157	            }
158	            msg
159	        }
160	        None => format!("device error: {line}"),
161	    }
162	}
163	
164	/// Parse the rate out of a `$VN(R|W)RG,07,<freq>*XX` response line.
165	/// Returns `None` for any other (e.g. async) line.
166	pub fn parse_reg07(line: &str) -> Option<u32> {
167	    let body = line
168	        .strip_prefix("$VNRRG,07,")
169	        .or_else(|| line.strip_prefix("$VNWRG,07,"))?;
170	    let freq = body.split('*').next()?;
171	    freq.trim().parse().ok()
172	}
173	
174	/// Parse register 6 (Async Data Output Type / ADOR) value from a reply.
175	pub fn parse_reg06(line: &str) -> Option<u8> {
176	    let body = line
177	        .strip_prefix("$VNRRG,06,")
178	        .or_else(|| line.strip_prefix("$VNWRG,06,"))?;
179	    body.split('*').next()?.trim().parse().ok()
180	}

186	pub fn vn_crc16(data: &[u8]) -> u16 {
```

```
56	pub fn lookup_field(name: &str) -> Option<&'static Field> {
57	    FIELDS.iter().find(|f| f.name == name)
58	}
59	
60	pub fn field_names() -> String {
61	    FIELDS.iter().map(|f| f.name).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")
62	}
63	
64	/// Parse a comma-separated `--fields` list into Common-group fields, ordered by
65	/// bit (the order the device emits them), de-duplicated.
66	pub fn parse_fields(list: &str) -> Result<Vec<&'static Field>, String> {
67	    let mut out: Vec<&'static Field> = Vec::new();
68	    for name in list.split(',').map(str::trim).filter(|s| !s.is_empty()) {
69	        let f = lookup_field(name)
70	            .ok_or_else(|| format!("unknown field `{name}`; choose from {}", field_names()))?;
71	        if !out.iter().any(|g| g.name == f.name) {
72	            out.push(f);
73	        }
74	    }
75	    if out.is_empty() {
76	        return Err("--fields needs at least one field".into());
77	    }
78	    out.sort_by_key(|f| f.bit);
79	    Ok(out)
80	}
81	
82	/// Default binary field set: timestamp + acceleration.
83	pub fn default_fields() -> Vec<&'static Field> {
84	    vec![
85	        lookup_field("time").unwrap(),
86	        lookup_field("accel").unwrap(),
87	    ]
88	}
```
